A. TSD Mission Moment – Troy High’s “Counseling Colts Care”  
The Troy High counselors have started a program working with all 9th grade 
students this year to help them feel welcome and at home at Troy High 
School.  We have invited the Troy High counseling team to this evening’s 
meeting to share a little more about this wonderful initiative.

BOARD MEETING   MINUTES OC TOBER 16, 2018        
SERVICES BUILDING  
President Schmidt called the meeting to order at 7:00 p.m. 
In  addition  to  Mr.  Schmidt,  present  were  Board  members 
Fleming,  Gottlieb,  Hammond,  Hauff,  and  Philippart.    Mr. 
Miletti was absent.  Four high school student representatives 
were  also  in  attendance.  Also  present  were  Superintendent 
Machesky;  administrators  Griffor,  Dixon,  Gottlieb,  Carrier, 
Harris, Dziatczak, and West; and approximately 75 additional 
members  of  the  audience.  Mr .  David  Youngstrom,  audit 
partner Yeo & Yeo Financial Services, was also in attendance. 
RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment – Protect 
Troy  Center  for  Transition.  Her  parents,  Dawn  and  Mark 
H a y e s ,  T r o y  t e a c h e r s ,  w e r e  i n v i t e d  u p  t o  s h a r e  t h e i r  
encouraging  story.  Students  at  Athens  High  School 
recognized an opportunity to live out the district’s mission 
of “All means All” by choosing Allie Hayes as Grand Marshall 
f o r  t h e i r  H o m e c o m i n g  P a r a d e  t h i s  y e a r .  T h e  e n t i r e  
community responded including faculty and students from 
Athens,  Troy  High,  para  educators  and  colleagues  from 
every  building,  multiple  media  outlets  and  Dr.  Machesky. 
The  day  Allie  was  Grand  Marshall,  her  family  witnessed  a 
restored  positive  self‐image  armed  with  courage  to 
continue to fight to protect all vulnerable adults.  The entire 
community  chose  to  stand  beside  Allie  in  the  parade 
wearing  yellow  shirts,  cheering,  waving  and  calling  her 
name.    It  endorsed  mutual  respect  and  empathy.    Allie’s 
parents  shared  a  brief  video  following  their  story.    Allie 
handed  out  yellow  tee‐shirts  and  cookies  to  Board 
members  then  received  a  certificate  of  recognition  and 
received congratulations from the Board.

comparable scores on the Scholastic Aptitude Test (SAT).

selection  as  2019  National  Merit  Scholarship  Semifinalists.

The meeting reconvened at 8:55 p.m.  
The meeting was adjourned at 9:40 p.m.

5. General – Consent Agenda 
E. CONSIDERATION:  CONSUMERS ENERGY COMPANY 
EASEMENT – BOULAN PARK MIDDLE SCHOOL 
Consumers Energy Company has requested an Easement on the Boulan 
Park Middle School Property. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: 
 Supported by:  
 WHEREAS, Consumers Energy Company  desires to construct, operate, 
maintain, inspect, survey, replace, reconstruct, improve, remove, relocate, 
change the size of, enlarge, prote ct a pipeline or pipelines in, on, under, over, 
across and through a portion of  a parcel of property owned by the Troy School 
District, the County of Oakland and the State of Michigan commonly known as 
Boulan Park Middle School (the “Property”); and 
 WHEREAS, in order to obtain access to the Property for the purposes 
stated above, the School District desires to grant to Consumers Energy Company 
a permanent easement on terms substantially similar to the terms and conditions 
set forth in the Easement Agreement attached hereto; and 
 WHEREAS, the Troy School District is desirous of autho rizing the 
Superintendent of the School District, Richard M. Machesky , Ed.D., to execute 
an easement agreement on terms substantially similar  to the terms set forth in 
the attached Easement Agreement; 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT:  The Superintendent of 
the School District, Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. is hereby authorized to execute 
an easement agreement with Consumers Energy Company on terms substantially 
similar to the terms set forth in the attached Easement Agreement.

EASEMENT FOR PIPELINES
Coolidge & Golf View Scada Pressure Monitor
SAP# 32537548
Master Tract# TRN000916037635
Agreement# MI00000038782
The School District of the City of Troy, of 4400 Livernois, Troy, Ml 48098 (hereinafter "Owner")
for $1.00 and other good and valuable consideration [exempt from real estate transfer tax pursuant to MCL 207.505(f) and 
from State real estate transfer tax pursuant to MCL 207.526(f)] grants and warrants to
CONSUMERS ENERGY COMPANY, a Michigan corporation, One Energy Plaza, Jackson, Michigan 49201 (hereinafter 
"Consumers")
a permanent non-exclusive easement to enter a portion of Owner's land (hereinafter "Owner's Land") located in the City of 
Troy, County of Oakland, and State of Michigan as more particularly described in the attached Exhibit A to construct, operate, 
maintain (including cathodic protection systems), inspect (including aerial patrol), survey, replace, reconstruct, improve, remove, 
relocate, change the size of, enlarge, protect, a pipeline or pipelines in, on, under, over, across, and through a portion of Owner's 
Land (hereinafter "Easement Area") as more fully described in the attached Exhibit B, together with any associated valves, 
fittings, location markers and signs, communication systems, utility lines, protective apparatus and all other equipment, 
appurtenances, and facilities, whether above or below grade, necessary, useful or incidental to or for the operation or protection 
thereof, and to conduct such other activities as may be necessary or convenient in connection therewith as determined by 
Consumers for the purpose of transmitting and distributing natural gas.
Additional Work Space: In addition to the Easement rights granted herein, Owner further grants to Consumers, during initial 
construction and installation only, the right to temporarily use such additional work space reasonably required to construct said 
pipelines as fully described in Exhibit C; provided that, such right does not unreasonably interrupt school operations on the 
Owner’s Land. Said temporary work space shall abut the Easement Area, on either side, as required by construction.
Access: Consumers shall have the right to unimpaired access to said Easement Area and pipeline or pipelines, and the right 
of ingress and egress on, over, and through Owner's Land, which access is more fully described in Exhibit D: provided that, 
such right does not unreasonable interrupt school operations on the Owner’s Land, for any and all purposes necessary, 
convenient, or incidental to the exercise by Consumers of the rights granted hereunder.
Trees and Other Vegetation: Owner shall not plant any trees within the Easement Area. Consumers shall have the right from 
time to time hereafter to enter Owner's Land to trim, cut down, and otherwise remove and control any trees, brush, roots, and 
other vegetation within the Easement Area.
Buildinqs/Structures: Owner agrees not to build, create, construct, or permit to be built, created, or constructed, any 
obstruction, building, septic system, drain field, fuel tank, pond, swimming pool, lake, pit, well, foundation, engineering works, 
installation or any other type of structure over, under, or on said Easement Area, whether temporary or permanent, natural or 
man-made, without a prior written agreement executed by Consumers' Real Estate Department expressly allowing the 
aforementioned.
Ground Elevation: Owner shall not materially alter the ground elevation within the Easement Area without a prior written 
agreement executed by Consumers Real Estate Department allowing said alteration.

120
121
Construction, Operation, Maintenance, Inspection, Repair, Replacement and Removal. The construction, operation, maintenance, 
inspection, repair, replacement, relocation, change the size of and/or removal of the pipeline or pipelines shall be at the sole cost and 
expense of Consumers and Consumers shall be responsible for constructing, operating, maintaining, inspecting, repairing and/or replacing 
the pipeline or pipelines in the condition required for its intended use. During all aspects of any work performed on the Owner’s Land, 
Consumers also covenants and agrees to do the following:
a) Backfill or fence during working hours all excavations on the Owner’s Land when not supervised to ensure safety;
b) Secure all equipment and materials during non-working hours so as to prevent access by any licensees, invitees, guests 
or trespassers;
c) Except in the case of emergency, Consumers shall use its best efforts not to block, impede or stall ingress and egress to 
Owner’s Land during construction and other activities on Owner’s Land.
Non-exclusive Easement. The easement, rights, and privileges granted herein are non-exclusive, and Owner reserves and retains the 
right to convey similar easements and rights to such other persons as Owner may deem proper provided such similar easements do not 
affect Consumer’s easement and only with Consumers written approval, which will not be unreasonably withheld.
Insurance. Consumers maintains excess liability insurance with substantial limits underwritten by Associated Electric and Gas Insurance 
Services, Ltd (AEGIS). This policy is subject to deductibles or self-insured retentions that are considered prudent and consistent with other 
utility companies of similar size and operations. Consumers agrees to maintain this policy while this Agreement remains in effect and will 
provide Troy School District with a copy of the Certificate of Insurance with Troy School District named additionally insured on the policy.
Liability of Consumers. Consumers shall indemnify, defend, and hold harmless Owner for liabilities incurred by the Owner, to the extent 
caused by the negligent actions of the Consumers during the construction, maintenance, inspection, repair, replacement, removal of the 
pipeline or pipelines and/or any such work performed upon on the Easement Area pursuant to this Easement. These liabilities shall 
include costs, expenses, and liabilities for personal injury or property damage, including damage to property of the Owner. The terms 
“Consumers” and “Owner” shall include their agents, contractors, successors and employees. Nothing in this clause shall be construed to 
limit the Owner’s defenses and rights, including the right to assert a claim of governmental immunity.
Covenants to Run with Land. The covenants contained in this Easement shall run with the land and shall be binding upon the parties and 
their respective assigns and successors.
Recording of Easement. This Easement for Pipelines shall be executed in recordable form and shall be recorded with the Oakland County 
Register of Deeds.
Restoration: Consumers shall restore as reasonably as practicable all that portion of Owner's Land damaged by Consumers during the 
original installation, maintenance, repair, replacement, or removal of Consumers' facilities on Owner's Land pursuant to this Easement to a 
condition existing immediately prior to the damage to Owner's Land; provided however, the provisions of this paragraph shall not apply to 
anything located within the Easement Area in violation of any other provisions contained in this Easement. If upon completion of any 
construction, operation, maintenance, inspection, repair, replacement, removal of the pipeline or pipelines and/or any such work performed 
upon the Owner’s Land, Owner’s Land has been affected in any manner by said construction, operation, maintenance, inspection, repair, 
replacement, removal of the pipeline or pipelines and/or any such work performed upon the Owner’s Land, Consumers shall, at its sole 
cost and expense, restore Owner’s Land to a condition as good as its condition prior to such work. Such restoration shall include but not 
be limited to the following:
a. The restoration of sodded and grassed areas;
b. Any driveways, parking lots, sidewalks, bike paths, culverts, curbs and headwalls so disturbed shall be restored with like 
materials and to matching thickness and appearance as prior to Consumer’s commencement of any such work;
c. Any and all shrubbery, removed, destroyed or disturbed in any manner shall be replaced upon completion of such work 
witn like shrubs; and
d. The restoration of disturbed or destroyed chainlink fencing, if any, located upon the Owner’s Land.
Such restoration shall occur not later than sixty (60) days after completion of construction, operation, maintenance, inspection, repair, 
replacement, removal of the pipeline or pipelines and/or any such work performed upon the Owner’s Land. If, however, weather 
conditions and/or local frost laws prevent the timely restoration of the Owner’s Land, such restoration shall occur not later than the first 
growing season after completion of the work performed.
Exercise of Easement: Consumers' nonuse or limited use of this Easement shall not preclude Consumers' later use of this 
Easement to its full extent.

Ownership: Owner covenants with Consumers that they are the lawful fee simple owner of the aforesaid lands, and that they 
have the right and authority to make this grant.
Successors: This Easement shall bind and benefit Owner's and Consumers' respective heirs, successors, lessees, licensees, 
and assigns.
Counterparts: This Easement may be executed simultaneously in two or more counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an 
original and all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument. It is not necessary that all parties execute any 
single counterpart if each party executes at least one counterpart.

  6. A. 11.20.18 
6. A.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
     Assistant Principal, Smith Middle School 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Ms. Kimberly Cornelissen to the 
position of Assistant Principal, Smith Middle School.   Ms. Cornelissen started her 
education career as fourth grade teacher at Wass Ele mentary.  She also served 
as a Summer Success Academy T eacher June 2014 through July 2016.  During 
her professional career , she has been a member of the Wass Elementary 
Leadership Team, attended Aspiring Princ ipals’ Leadership Academy at Oakland 
Schools as a Troy Schoo l District Representative, and has acted as a Continuous 
School Improvement Team Member for Wass Elementary.   Ms. Cornelissen has a 
Bachelor of Arts in Special Education  from Michigan State University and a  
Master of Arts in K-12 Educational Administration from Michigan State University.  
With your approval, Ms. Cornelissen will begin h er duties as Assistant Principal, 
Smith Middle School as of Monday, December 3, 2018.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education issues a two-year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Ms. Kimberly Cornelissen, who will begin her duties as Assistant Principal, 
Smith Middle School effective December 3, 2018.

8. BUSINESS 
A.  CONSIDERATION: Troy High School Sign Refurbishment 
A RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for the refurbishment of the 
school messaging sign at Troy High School .  Bid responses were 
reviewed by Rob Carson, Director of Operations and it is recommended 
the bid be awarded as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the bid for Troy High  School sign 
refurbishment be awarded to Signarama of Troy  in the amount of 
$21,739.30 and that th is amount be approved as an expenditure of the  
2016 Capital Projects Fund. 
 Yes: 
 No: 
132
Memorandum 
TO:  Rick West – Asst. Superintendent of Business Services 
Operations 
Department 
FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations   
1140 Rankin 
Ph: 248.823.4050 
Fax: 248.823.4077 
DATE:  November 8, 2018 
An RFP (9866) was created to obtain competitive b ids for the refurbishment of the 
school messaging sign  at Troy High School.   The scope of work for this RFP is to 
provide labor & materials to replace the electronic message center, school logos and 
aluminum skirting on the school sign (Long Lake entrance)  at Troy High S chool as 
detailed in the bid specifications.   
On October 30 th 5 bids were open ed which resulted in  Signarama of Troy  being 
identified as the low qualified bidder (please see attached bid tally).  Signarama’s bid 
has been reviewed and I am recommending they be awarded the project work. 
I am re questing a total project cost of $21,739 .30 which consists of the base bid of 
$19,763.00 and 10% Contingency $1,976.30.  The funding for this purchase wi ll be 
from the 2016 Capital Projects Fund. 
Please let me know if you have any questions.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department  
 Purchasing Department – File

8. BUSINESS 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   PURCHASE AGREEMENT Boyd St. Property 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Approve the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
 WHEREAS, the Scho ol District desires to sell the parcel of land  with the 
following legal description: 
THE EASTERLY 6.00 FEET OF LOT 27 , “BEAVER RUN SUBDIVISION”, A PART OF THE 
SOUTHWEST ¼ OF SECTION 23 , TOWN 2 NORTH, RANGE 11 EAST, CITY OF TROY, 
OAKLAND COUNTY, MICHIGAN, AS RECORDED IN LIBER 46, OF PLOTS ON PAGE 2 7, 
OAKLAND COUNTY RECORDS. 
  and 
 WHEREAS, the School District has received an Offer To Purchase this property 
from, Delmar Homes (the “Purchaser”), to purchase the Premises for a purchase price 
of Ten Thousand and 00/100 ($10,000.00) Dollars, (the “Purchase Price”); and 
 WHEREAS, the S chool District has reviewed the Offer and is desirous of 
accepting said Offer and authorizing the sale of the Premises on terms substantially 
similar to those described in the Offer, which Offer is attached hereto and incorporated 
herein by reference as Exhibit A.   
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The School District authorizes the sale of the Premises on terms 
substantially similar to the terms contained in the attached Offer. 
2. The Superintendent of the School District, or his designee, is hereby 
authorized to execute the Offer and any and all documents necessary 
and incidental to the sale of the Premises on behalf of the School District. 
YES: MEMBERS:
